It's just the getting stuck and the repeating my own movements, like I told you about the napkins. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating the same words or phrases over and over again, even if someone else didn’t say them first?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think on that... no, I haven't noticed myself doing that. Or I'll just stand there folding the same stack of napkins over and over again, even when people are waiting for me to finish. It's just... strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your body is frozen or that you can’t move, even though you want to? Or do you sometimes repeat movements or actions without meaning to?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor, that's exactly what I was just trying to say. Like with that church potluck I mentioned... I'll just get completely stuck. I'll be holding that serving spoon up in the air and my body just won't move, even though I want to put it down. \n\nAnd it's the same with the napkins. I don't mean to keep doing it, but my hands just keep folding that same stack over and over again while folks are standing right there waiting on me. So yes, it's just like you said... I either freeze up or just keep repeating things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself imitating someone else's movements without meaning to, like copying what someone else is doing?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. I don't think I've ever just copied what someone else was doing like that. It's really just the freezing up and the repeating things, like I was saying before.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed yourself doing something like mirroring someone else’s movements without realizing it—like if someone waved their hand, would you do the same without meaning to?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a second ago... no, I haven't noticed anything like that. If somebody waves at me, I just wave back because I mean to, not by accident. I don't mirror folks. It really is just the getting stuck and repeating things, like I mentioned before.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself doing something, like touching your face or moving your hands, without realizing it—especially after someone else did the same thing?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we're going in circles here. Like I said before, no. I don't copy what other people are doing. If someone touches their face, I don't just touch mine without thinking about it. I don't do anything like that. It's just the getting stuck and the repeating my own movements, like I told you about the napkins. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating the same words or phrases over and over again, even if someone else didn’t say them first?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think on that... no, I haven't noticed myself doing that. I don't just say the same words over and over. Like I was saying before, it's really just my hands getting stuck, or doing the same physical motion with the napkins. But talking? No, my words don't get stuck like that. I just talk regular.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating someone else’s exact words or phrases without intending to, like if someone says something and you just say it back automatically?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't done that either. If somebody says something to me, I just answer them back normal. I don't repeat their words back to them like a parrot or anything. It's really just the physical things with my hands, like I've been saying. My speech is just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself making movements that seem to happen on their own, like twitching or moving in ways that don’t have a clear purpose or goal?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I don't have any twitches or... jerks or anything like that. My body doesn't just move all on its own for no reason. Like I said, it's really just what I told you about before, with my hands getting stuck doing the same thing with the napkins. But just random twitching? No, I haven't had any of that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself holding your body in unusual positions for long periods of time, or making repeated movements that seem to have no purpose, like rocking back and forth or waving your arms in a strange way?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor, that's... that's exactly why I came in, to be honest. It's like what I was saying about the napkins. \n\nAt our church, I just keep doing these odd things. I'll be standing right by the potluck table, and I'll just hold the serving spoon up in the air and... well, I just don't move. I get stuck holding it. Or I'll stand there folding that exact same stack of napkins over and over again, even with people standing right there waiting on me to finish. It's just... it's not right, and I don't know why I do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your body stays in one position even when someone tries to move you, like if someone tried to move your arm, it would just stay where they put it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Nobody's really tried to move my arm or anything when I get stuck like that. I just stand there holding the spoon or folding the napkins until I finally snap out of it. So I really couldn't say if my arm would stay where they put it, because it hasn't happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself imitating someone else’s movements or actions without realizing it or meaning to?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't... I don't copy what other folks are doing. It's really just me getting stuck doing my own thing, like I was saying before with the napkins. But catching myself imitating somebody else's movements? No, I haven't done that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced times when your body felt completely immobile for a long period, like you couldn't move at all even though you wanted to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had that happen. I mean, when I get stuck holding things, it's... it's not like I'm trying to move and my body just won't let me. I'm just sort of paused there doing it. I've never felt completely frozen or trapped when I actually wanted to walk away or move around. So, no, not like what you're saying.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other times, outside of the church setting, where your body does repetitive or purposeless actions without you meaning for it to?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. It's really just been... well, just those times I mentioned, like with the napkins or the spoon. I haven't noticed myself doing anything else like that just out of nowhere, not anywhere else. Just those specific things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself not responding to instructions or questions, even though you understood them, or felt like you had to follow someone else's instructions without question?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, if someone asks me a question or tells me to do something, I answer them or I do it. I haven't ever just sat there and ignored them when I knew what they were saying. And I don't feel like I have to do whatever someone tells me, either. I just... I just go about my day normal, mostly. So no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating the exact same words someone else just said, even if you didn’t mean to?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I just... I talk normal. If somebody says something to me, I just answer them back with my own words. I've never caught myself just repeating back what they said like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

No, I haven't had any of that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself holding your body in unusual positions for long periods of time, or making repeated movements that seem to have no purpose, like rocking back and forth or waving your arms in a strange way?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor, that's... that's exactly why I came in, to be honest. It's like what I was saying about the napkins. \n\nAt our church, I just keep doing these odd things. I'll be standing right by the potluck table, and I'll just hold the serving spoon up in the air and... well, I just don't move. I get stuck holding it. Or I'll stand there folding that exact same stack of napkins over and over again, even with people standing right there waiting on me to finish. It's just... it's not right, and I don't know why I do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your body stays in one position even when someone tries to move you, like if someone tried to move your arm, it would just stay where they put it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Nobody's really tried to move my arm or anything when I get stuck like that. I just stand there holding the spoon or folding the napkins until I finally snap out of it. So I really couldn't say if my arm would stay where they put it, because it hasn't happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself imitating someone else’s movements or actions without realizing it or meaning to?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't... I don't copy what other folks are doing. It's really just me getting stuck doing my own thing, like I was saying before with the napkins. But catching myself imitating somebody else's movements? No, I haven't done that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced times when your body felt completely immobile for a long period, like you couldn't move at all even though you wanted to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had that happen. I mean, when I get stuck holding things, it's... it's not like I'm trying to move and my body just won't let me. I'm just sort of paused there doing it. I've never felt completely frozen or trapped when I actually wanted to walk away or move around. So, no, not like what you're saying.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other times, outside of the church setting, where your body does repetitive or purposeless actions without you meaning for it to?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. It's really just been... well, just those times I mentioned, like with the napkins or the spoon. I haven't noticed myself doing anything else like that just out of nowhere, not anywhere else. Just those specific things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself not responding to instructions or questions, even though you understood them, or felt like you had to follow someone else's instructions without question?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, if someone asks me a question or tells me to do something, I answer them or I do it. I haven't ever just sat there and ignored them when I knew what they were saying. And I don't feel like I have to do whatever someone tells me, either. I just... I just go about my day normal, mostly. So no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating the exact same words someone else just said, even if you didn’t mean to?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I just... I talk normal. If somebody says something to me, I just answer them back with my own words. I've never caught myself just repeating back what they said like that.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: How long have you been experiencing these episodes of freezing up and repeating movements—have they been happening for more than six months now?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's been right about six months. That's when all this odd behavior started, like the freezing up. And... well, my talking, too. People tell me I'm hard to follow sometimes. But yes, it's been noticeable for about the past six months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It really is just the getting stuck and repeating things, like I mentioned before.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself doing something, like touching your face or moving your hands, without realizing it—especially after someone else did the same thing?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we're going in circles here. Like I said before, no. I don't copy what other people are doing. If someone touches their face, I don't just touch mine without thinking about it. I don't do anything like that. It's just the getting stuck and the repeating my own movements, like I told you about the napkins. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating the same words or phrases over and over again, even if someone else didn’t say them first?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think on that... no, I haven't noticed myself doing that. No, my words don't get stuck like that. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I came in because... well, it's been happening at church lately. I just keep doing odd things. Like, last week, I was standing by the potluck table, just holding the serving spoon up in the air, and I couldn't seem to move. Or I'll just stand there folding the same stack of napkins over and over again, even when people are waiting for me to finish. It's just... strange.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your body is frozen or that you can’t move, even though you want to? Or do you sometimes repeat movements or actions without meaning to?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor, that's exactly what I was just trying to say. Like with that church potluck I mentioned... I'll just get completely stuck. I'll be holding that serving spoon up in the air and my body just won't move, even though I want to put it down. \n\nAnd it's the same with the napkins. I don't mean to keep doing it, but my hands just keep folding that same stack over and over again while folks are standing right there waiting on me. So yes, it's just like you said... I either freeze up or just keep repeating things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself imitating someone else's movements without meaning to, like copying what someone else is doing?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. I don't think I've ever just copied what someone else was doing like that. It's really just the freezing up and the repeating things, like I was saying before.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed yourself doing something like mirroring someone else’s movements without realizing it—like if someone waved their hand, would you do the same without meaning to?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a second ago... no, I haven't noticed anything like that. If somebody waves at me, I just wave back because I mean to, not by accident. I don't mirror folks. It really is just the getting stuck and repeating things, like I mentioned before.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself doing something, like touching your face or moving your hands, without realizing it—especially after someone else did the same thing?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we're going in circles here. Like I said before, no. I don't copy what other people are doing. If someone touches their face, I don't just touch mine without thinking about it. I don't do anything like that. It's just the getting stuck and the repeating my own movements, like I told you about the napkins. That's all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating the same words or phrases over and over again, even if someone else didn’t say them first?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think on that... no, I haven't noticed myself doing that. I don't just say the same words over and over. Like I was saying before, it's really just my hands getting stuck, or doing the same physical motion with the napkins. But talking? No, my words don't get stuck like that. I just talk regular.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating someone else’s exact words or phrases without intending to, like if someone says something and you just say it back automatically?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't done that either. If somebody says something to me, I just answer them back normal. I don't repeat their words back to them like a parrot or anything. It's really just the physical things with my hands, like I've been saying. My speech is just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself making movements that seem to happen on their own, like twitching or moving in ways that don’t have a clear purpose or goal?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I don't have any twitches or... jerks or anything like that. My body doesn't just move all on its own for no reason. Like I said, it's really just what I told you about before, with my hands getting stuck doing the same thing with the napkins. But just random twitching? No, I haven't had any of that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself holding your body in unusual positions for long periods of time, or making repeated movements that seem to have no purpose, like rocking back and forth or waving your arms in a strange way?\n\nPatient: Yes, doctor, that's... that's exactly why I came in, to be honest. It's like what I was saying about the napkins. \n\nAt our church, I just keep doing these odd things. I'll be standing right by the potluck table, and I'll just hold the serving spoon up in the air and... well, I just don't move. I get stuck holding it. Or I'll stand there folding that exact same stack of napkins over and over again, even with people standing right there waiting on me to finish. It's just... it's not right, and I don't know why I do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your body stays in one position even when someone tries to move you, like if someone tried to move your arm, it would just stay where they put it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Nobody's really tried to move my arm or anything when I get stuck like that. I just stand there holding the spoon or folding the napkins until I finally snap out of it. So I really couldn't say if my arm would stay where they put it, because it hasn't happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself imitating someone else’s movements or actions without realizing it or meaning to?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I haven't noticed anything like that. I don't... I don't copy what other folks are doing. It's really just me getting stuck doing my own thing, like I was saying before with the napkins. But catching myself imitating somebody else's movements? No, I haven't done that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced times when your body felt completely immobile for a long period, like you couldn't move at all even though you wanted to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had that happen. I mean, when I get stuck holding things, it's... it's not like I'm trying to move and my body just won't let me. I'm just sort of paused there doing it. I've never felt completely frozen or trapped when I actually wanted to walk away or move around. So, no, not like what you're saying.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other times, outside of the church setting, where your body does repetitive or purposeless actions without you meaning for it to?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. It's really just been... well, just those times I mentioned, like with the napkins or the spoon. I haven't noticed myself doing anything else like that just out of nowhere, not anywhere else. Just those specific things.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself not responding to instructions or questions, even though you understood them, or felt like you had to follow someone else's instructions without question?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, if someone asks me a question or tells me to do something, I answer them or I do it. I haven't ever just sat there and ignored them when I knew what they were saying. And I don't feel like I have to do whatever someone tells me, either. I just... I just go about my day normal, mostly. So no, nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating the exact same words someone else just said, even if you didn’t mean to?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I just... I talk normal. If somebody says something to me, I just answer them back with my own words. I've never caught myself just repeating back what they said like that.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: How long have you been experiencing these episodes of freezing up and repeating movements—have they been happening for more than six months now?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's been right about six months. That's when all this odd behavior started, like the freezing up. And... well, my talking, too. People tell me I'm hard to follow sometimes. But yes, it's been noticeable for about the past six months.\n\nDoctor: Are these episodes of freezing up and repeating movements something you experience every day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really know for sure. I just know it's been going on for a while now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating the exact same words someone else just said, even if you didn’t mean to?\n\nPatient: I think you just asked me that one, doctor. But no, like I said a minute ago, I don't do that. I just use my own words when I talk to folks. I've never caught myself repeating what somebody else just said.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens right before one of these episodes starts—do you feel a strong urge to repeat the movement, or does it happen suddenly without any warning?\n\nPatient: Well, to tell you the truth, I'm not really sure. I don't feel any kind of strong urge or anything like that before it starts. It just... well, it just seems to happen out of nowhere, I suppose.
